========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, honestly, I didn’t even really want to come in today, but... well, my husband basically made me. He and the people at the warehouse where I work, they just keep acting like I’m crazy. Like I'm making things up or losing my mind. But I'm not. I work freight, you know, moving materials, loading and unloading the trucks, and I’ve been busting my back there for a long time. I know how the floor runs better than anyone. 

There's this supervisor spot that opened up, and I know I deserve it. But... see, the thing is, I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel them doing it. Every time I'm out on the floor with the pallet jack, they're keeping tabs on me, standing just out of sight by the loading docks. And the worst part—the part my husband just refuses to understand even when I try to explain the math to him—is that they are actually changing my pallet counts. 

I count every single pallet I wrap and move. I know exactly what my numbers are. But at the end of the shift, the logs are always short. They are purposely going in and changing the counts to make my production look bad, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. I tried telling my husband how the guys on the floor are always whispering when I walk by, how they're coordinating the whole thing to sabotage me, but he just looks at me with this... this look, like I'm the one who's got a problem. I don't have a problem, I just pay attention. If you worked in a warehouse, you'd know how cutthroat it gets, and they know I'd be their boss if they didn't mess with my numbers, so they're all in on it together...

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I haven't caught them red-handed at the computer, if that's what you mean, but I don't need to. I know my numbers. I mean, think about it. You don't just lose track of a whole pallet of goods, right? It's not a misplaced pen. We're talking hundreds of pounds of freight. I stack it, I wrap it, I tag it, I move it. I count every single one. I even started keeping a little tally in my pocket, just making a mark with a Sharpie on a piece of scrap cardboard every time I drop one at the loading dock. I do it discreetly, of course, because if they knew I was keeping my own records, they'd probably steal the cardboard right out of my vest. 

So, I have my tally, right? I know I moved, say, forty-two pallets by lunch. But then the shift report comes out, and it says I moved thirty-eight. Where did the other four go? Did they just evaporate? No, of course not. The system doesn't just glitch like that, not every single day, and not just for me. It's so obvious what's happening. The guys on the floor—specifically Dave and Marcus, they're always hanging around the breakroom when I'm out sweating on the floor—they're the ones doing it. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. 

And my husband, he just says, "Maybe you miscounted, honey," or "Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right." But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. They want that supervisor job for themselves, or for one of their buddies, and they know the only way to stop me from getting it is to make it look like I'm slacking off and losing my mind. But I'm not. I'm just paying attention to what's right in front of my face. I mean, the math doesn't lie, right? My tally marks don't lie. They are definitely changing the counts. I just know it.

The guys on the floor—specifically Dave and Marcus, they're always hanging around the breakroom when I'm out sweating on the floor—they're the ones doing it. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. \n\nAnd my husband, he just says, \"Maybe you miscounted, honey,\" or \"Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right.\" But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. So yeah, they are treating me differently because of what I've accomplished and what I'm capable of. 

That's why they're changing my pallet counts. They have to. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. Because if they don't mess with the system, if they don't actively sabotage my production logs, I'm getting that promotion. And Dave and Marcus, they can't stand the thought of taking orders from me. They want one of their own buddies in that spot, someone who will let them take extra-long smoke breaks and slack off by the water cooler. So they target me. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. It's so clear to me, I just don't understand why no one else will just look at the facts and see it!

Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 6 months\n  manifestation: The stretches where I feel wired, sleep very little, and take on too much have been coming and going for about 6 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, I didn’t even really want to come in today, but... well, my husband basically made me. He and the people at the warehouse where I work, they just keep acting like I’m crazy. Like I'm making things up or losing my mind. But I'm not. I work freight, you know, moving materials, loading and unloading the trucks, and I’ve been busting my back there for a long time. I know how the floor runs better than anyone. \n\nThere's this supervisor spot that opened up, and I know I deserve it. But... see, the thing is, I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel them doing it. Every time I'm out on the floor with the pallet jack, they're keeping tabs on me, standing just out of sight by the loading docks. And the worst part—the part my husband just refuses to understand even when I try to explain the math to him—is that they are actually changing my pallet counts. \n\nI count every single pallet I wrap and move. I know exactly what my numbers are. But at the end of the shift, the logs are always short. They are purposely going in and changing the counts to make my production look bad, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. I tried telling my husband how the guys on the floor are always whispering when I walk by, how they're coordinating the whole thing to sabotage me, but he just looks at me with this... this look, like I'm the one who's got a problem. I don't have a problem, I just pay attention. If you worked in a warehouse, you'd know how cutthroat it gets, and they know I'd be their boss if they didn't mess with my numbers, so they're all in on it together...\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know they are changing your pallet counts? Have you seen anyone actually doing it, or is it based on your observations and feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't caught them red-handed at the computer, if that's what you mean, but I don't need to. I know my numbers. I mean, think about it. You don't just lose track of a whole pallet of goods, right? It's not a misplaced pen. We're talking hundreds of pounds of freight. I stack it, I wrap it, I tag it, I move it. I count every single one. I even started keeping a little tally in my pocket, just making a mark with a Sharpie on a piece of scrap cardboard every time I drop one at the loading dock. I do it discreetly, of course, because if they knew I was keeping my own records, they'd probably steal the cardboard right out of my vest. \n\nSo, I have my tally, right? I know I moved, say, forty-two pallets by lunch. But then the shift report comes out, and it says I moved thirty-eight. Where did the other four go? Did they just evaporate? No, of course not. The system doesn't just glitch like that, not every single day, and not just for me. It's so obvious what's happening. The guys on the floor—specifically Dave and Marcus, they're always hanging around the breakroom when I'm out sweating on the floor—they're the ones doing it. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. \n\nAnd my husband, he just says, \"Maybe you miscounted, honey,\" or \"Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right.\" But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. They want that supervisor job for themselves, or for one of their buddies, and they know the only way to stop me from getting it is to make it look like I'm slacking off and losing my mind. But I'm not. I'm just paying attention to what's right in front of my face. I mean, the math doesn't lie, right? My tally marks don't lie. They are definitely changing the counts. I just know it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people at work are targeting you specifically or treating you differently because of who you are or what you've accomplished?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. But I know for a fact they are watching my every move out on that floor. It's not just a feeling, it's a coordinated effort. \n\nThey know I'm the hardest worker there. I've been doing this since I dropped out in the 11th grade—I know freight, I know how to move material, I know the flow of the dock better than the managers do. And they know that if it was just based on merit, if it was just based on who actually gets the work done, that supervisor spot would be mine in a heartbeat. So yeah, they are treating me differently because of what I've accomplished and what I'm capable of. \n\nThat's why they're changing my pallet counts. They have to. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. Because if they don't mess with the system, if they don't actively sabotage my production logs, I'm getting that promotion. And Dave and Marcus, they can't stand the thought of taking orders from me. They want one of their own buddies in that spot, someone who will let them take extra-long smoke breaks and slack off by the water cooler. So they target me. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. It's so clear to me, I just don't understand why no one else will just look at the facts and see it!\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others around you don’t seem to notice, like voices, visions, or other experiences that aren’t there for other people?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Well, I mean... it is funny you ask that. Because, see, I do not really like talking about this part. It just gives my husband more ammunition, you know? Like, if I tell him, he will just use it against me and say, see, Nicole, I told you that you are losing it. But... I mean, I guess there is this one thing. It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. 

It is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. 

The first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.

It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. \n\nIt is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. \n\nThe first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"Sometimes when I'm in the aisle or folding clothes at home, I hear a man's voice clear as day saying, \"Nicole, check it again,\" even when nobody is near me.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. And it is always that same man's voice, clear as day, telling me, Nicole, check it again. 

It is so real that I physically turn my whole body around because I expect to bump right into whoever is standing there trying to sneak up on me. And then, like I mentioned, even when I am just at home folding clothes in the living room, it happens the exact same way. It sounds like it is coming from right behind the couch, or just hovering over my shoulder while I am matching up my husband's work socks. And there is nobody near me! Nobody at all. I am completely alone in the room, but the voice is right there in the air. 

If it was just inside my head, why would I be looking behind the laundry basket? Why would I be walking to the end of the warehouse aisle to see who just spoke to me? My husband tries to tell me it is just my inner monologue working overtime because I am stressed out about Dave and Marcus messing with my pallet counts, but your inner monologue does not echo off the metal racking in a warehouse, does it? No, it does not. It is an external sound, completely outside of my body, just hanging there in the room, and it honestly just frustrates me so much that nobody else can hear it when it is that loud and that close to my own two ears.

Manic Episode Duration\n  description: 6 months\n  manifestation: The stretches where I feel wired, sleep very little, and take on too much have been coming and going for about 6 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S019\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you believe that people at work are targeting you specifically or treating you differently because of who you are or what you've accomplished?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. But I know for a fact they are watching my every move out on that floor. It's not just a feeling, it's a coordinated effort. \n\nThey know I'm the hardest worker there. I've been doing this since I dropped out in the 11th grade—I know freight, I know how to move material, I know the flow of the dock better than the managers do. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. Like, if I tell him, he will just use it against me and say, see, Nicole, I told you that you are losing it. But... I mean, I guess there is this one thing. It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. \n\nIt is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. \n\nThe first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. And it is always that same man's voice, clear as day, telling me, Nicole, check it again. \n\nIt is so real that I physically turn my whole body around because I expect to bump right into whoever is standing there trying to sneak up on me. And then, like I mentioned, even when I am just at home folding clothes in the living room, it happens the exact same way. It sounds like it is coming from right behind the couch, or just hovering over my shoulder while I am matching up my husband's work socks. And there is nobody near me! Nobody at all. I am completely alone in the room, but the voice is right there in the air. \n\nIf it was just inside my head, why would I be looking behind the laundry basket? Why would I be walking to the end of the warehouse aisle to see who just spoke to me? No, it does not. It is an external sound, completely outside of my body, just hanging there in the room, and it honestly just frustrates me so much that nobody else can hear it when it is that loud and that close to my own two ears.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had any other experiences where you believed something was happening or someone was communicating with you that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I mean... no, not really. Just the voice, just that man telling me to check it again. I pay attention to my surroundings because you have to when you're moving heavy freight all day. If you don't pay attention, you get run over by a forklift or you drop a pallet of glass jars and spend three hours sweeping it up. So I notice things. I notice when people are whispering, I notice when my counts are off, and I notice when a man speaks right into my ear. 

But it's not like I'm sitting around trying to decode hidden meanings in the breakroom bulletin board. I just go to work, I do my job better than anyone else there, and I try to keep my head down until they force me to defend myself. I don't have time to be making things up or looking for messages that aren't there. Between the warehouse and trying to keep the house clean while my husband just sits there telling me I'm crazy, I'm exhausted. So no, just the voice and the sabotage at work. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. I'm very observant, yes. I pay attention to my surroundings because you have to when you're moving heavy freight all day. If you don't pay attention, you get run over by a forklift or you drop a pallet of glass jars and spend three hours sweeping it up. So I notice things. I notice when people are whispering, I notice when my counts are off, and I notice when a man speaks right into my ear. \n\nBut it's not like I'm sitting around trying to decode hidden meanings in the breakroom bulletin board. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, that is exactly why I am sitting in this chair right now, honestly. I came in because my husband and the warehouse people, they all keep acting like I am completely crazy. Like I am just making things up out of thin air or losing my grip on reality. But I am not. I know exactly what is going on, and I know for an absolute fact that they have been watching my every single move at work. 

You have to understand how a warehouse operates, okay? It is all about your numbers. How much freight you move, how accurate your logs are, how fast you clear the loading dock. And I work harder than anyone else on that floor. I do not stand around drinking energy drinks and gossiping like Dave and Marcus do. I keep my head down and I pull my pallets. And because of that, there is this supervisor spot that is opening up soon. It is a big deal, it means a pay bump, it means better hours, and it means I would actually be in charge of the floor. And they know I deserve it. They know I am the top candidate. 

So, what do they do? They start sabotaging me. I know they have been sneaking around and changing my pallet counts to make me look incompetent. I will count a pallet of freight, log it perfectly, wrap it, and put it in the racks. Then, two hours later, the floor manager comes up to me and says my count is short by three boxes. And I know it wasn't short! I know they went in and changed the tags or moved the boxes just to mess up my accuracy rate. They are doing it specifically to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, because they want it for themselves or they just cannot stand the idea of me telling them what to do.

But when I try to explain this to my husband, when I tell him that there is this whole coordinated effort against me at work, he just sighs and rolls his eyes at me. He tells me I am being paranoid. He says, Nicole, there is no proof of that, you are just stressed, you are imagining things, why would they risk their jobs just to mess with your pallets? And it makes me so angry because the proof is right there! The proof is that my numbers are suddenly wrong when they have never been wrong before in my life! But he just sides with them, basically. He tells me I need help, that my beliefs are not real, and he acts like I am this crazy person who needs to be fixed. But I am not crazy. I just have coworkers who are actively trying to ruin my livelihood, and a husband who refuses to open his eyes and see it.

I'm Dr. [Name]. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly, I didn’t even really want to come in today, but... well, my husband basically made me. He and the people at the warehouse where I work, they just keep acting like I’m crazy. Like I'm making things up or losing my mind. But I'm not. I work freight, you know, moving materials, loading and unloading the trucks, and I’ve been busting my back there for a long time. I know how the floor runs better than anyone. \n\nThere's this supervisor spot that opened up, and I know I deserve it. But... see, the thing is, I know they’ve been watching my moves. I can feel them doing it. Every time I'm out on the floor with the pallet jack, they're keeping tabs on me, standing just out of sight by the loading docks. And the worst part—the part my husband just refuses to understand even when I try to explain the math to him—is that they are actually changing my pallet counts. \n\nI count every single pallet I wrap and move. I know exactly what my numbers are. But at the end of the shift, the logs are always short. They are purposely going in and changing the counts to make my production look bad, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. I tried telling my husband how the guys on the floor are always whispering when I walk by, how they're coordinating the whole thing to sabotage me, but he just looks at me with this... this look, like I'm the one who's got a problem. I don't have a problem, I just pay attention. If you worked in a warehouse, you'd know how cutthroat it gets, and they know I'd be their boss if they didn't mess with my numbers, so they're all in on it together...\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know they are changing your pallet counts? Have you seen anyone actually doing it, or is it based on your observations and feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't caught them red-handed at the computer, if that's what you mean, but I don't need to. I know my numbers. I mean, think about it. You don't just lose track of a whole pallet of goods, right? It's not a misplaced pen. We're talking hundreds of pounds of freight. I stack it, I wrap it, I tag it, I move it. I count every single one. I even started keeping a little tally in my pocket, just making a mark with a Sharpie on a piece of scrap cardboard every time I drop one at the loading dock. I do it discreetly, of course, because if they knew I was keeping my own records, they'd probably steal the cardboard right out of my vest. \n\nSo, I have my tally, right? I know I moved, say, forty-two pallets by lunch. But then the shift report comes out, and it says I moved thirty-eight. Where did the other four go? Did they just evaporate? No, of course not. The system doesn't just glitch like that, not every single day, and not just for me. It's so obvious what's happening. The guys on the floor—specifically Dave and Marcus, they're always hanging around the breakroom when I'm out sweating on the floor—they're the ones doing it. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. \n\nAnd my husband, he just says, \"Maybe you miscounted, honey,\" or \"Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right.\" But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. They want that supervisor job for themselves, or for one of their buddies, and they know the only way to stop me from getting it is to make it look like I'm slacking off and losing my mind. But I'm not. I'm just paying attention to what's right in front of my face. I mean, the math doesn't lie, right? My tally marks don't lie. They are definitely changing the counts. I just know it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people at work are targeting you specifically or treating you differently because of who you are or what you've accomplished?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. But I know for a fact they are watching my every move out on that floor. It's not just a feeling, it's a coordinated effort. \n\nThey know I'm the hardest worker there. I've been doing this since I dropped out in the 11th grade—I know freight, I know how to move material, I know the flow of the dock better than the managers do. And they know that if it was just based on merit, if it was just based on who actually gets the work done, that supervisor spot would be mine in a heartbeat. So yeah, they are treating me differently because of what I've accomplished and what I'm capable of. \n\nThat's why they're changing my pallet counts. They have to. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. Because if they don't mess with the system, if they don't actively sabotage my production logs, I'm getting that promotion. And Dave and Marcus, they can't stand the thought of taking orders from me. They want one of their own buddies in that spot, someone who will let them take extra-long smoke breaks and slack off by the water cooler. So they target me. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. It's so clear to me, I just don't understand why no one else will just look at the facts and see it!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others around you don’t seem to notice, like voices, visions, or other experiences that aren’t there for other people?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... it is funny you ask that. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. \n\nAnd my husband, he just says, \"Maybe you miscounted, honey,\" or \"Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right.\" But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. They want that supervisor job for themselves, or for one of their buddies, and they know the only way to stop me from getting it is to make it look like I'm slacking off and losing my mind. But I'm not. I'm just paying attention to what's right in front of my face. I mean, the math doesn't lie, right? My tally marks don't lie. They are definitely changing the counts. I just know it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people at work are targeting you specifically or treating you differently because of who you are or what you've accomplished?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. But I know for a fact they are watching my every move out on that floor. It's not just a feeling, it's a coordinated effort. \n\nThey know I'm the hardest worker there. I've been doing this since I dropped out in the 11th grade—I know freight, I know how to move material, I know the flow of the dock better than the managers do. And they know that if it was just based on merit, if it was just based on who actually gets the work done, that supervisor spot would be mine in a heartbeat. So yeah, they are treating me differently because of what I've accomplished and what I'm capable of. \n\nThat's why they're changing my pallet counts. They have to. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. Because if they don't mess with the system, if they don't actively sabotage my production logs, I'm getting that promotion. And Dave and Marcus, they can't stand the thought of taking orders from me. They want one of their own buddies in that spot, someone who will let them take extra-long smoke breaks and slack off by the water cooler. So they target me. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. It's so clear to me, I just don't understand why no one else will just look at the facts and see it!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others around you don’t seem to notice, like voices, visions, or other experiences that aren’t there for other people?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... it is funny you ask that. Because, see, I do not really like talking about this part. It just gives my husband more ammunition, you know? Like, if I tell him, he will just use it against me and say, see, Nicole, I told you that you are losing it. But... I mean, I guess there is this one thing. It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. \n\nIt is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. \n\nThe first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. And it is always that same man's voice, clear as day, telling me, Nicole, check it again. \n\nIt is so real that I physically turn my whole body around because I expect to bump right into whoever is standing there trying to sneak up on me. And then, like I mentioned, even when I am just at home folding clothes in the living room, it happens the exact same way. It sounds like it is coming from right behind the couch, or just hovering over my shoulder while I am matching up my husband's work socks. And there is nobody near me! Nobody at all. I am completely alone in the room, but the voice is right there in the air. \n\nIf it was just inside my head, why would I be looking behind the laundry basket? Why would I be walking to the end of the warehouse aisle to see who just spoke to me? My husband tries to tell me it is just my inner monologue working overtime because I am stressed out about Dave and Marcus messing with my pallet counts, but your inner monologue does not echo off the metal racking in a warehouse, does it? No, it does not. It is an external sound, completely outside of my body, just hanging there in the room, and it honestly just frustrates me so much that nobody else can hear it when it is that loud and that close to my own two ears.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences where you believed something was happening or someone was communicating with you that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Just the voice, just that man telling me to check it again. And the thing with Dave and Marcus messing with my numbers, but that's not... that's not me believing something that isn't happening, that's just a fact. That's just them being threatened by my work ethic. But as far as other things... no. I don't get secret messages from the TV or the radio or anything like that, if that's what you mean. \n\nI know some people get like that, where they think the news anchor is talking directly to them or they see signs in the license plates on the highway, but I'm not like that. I'm very observant, yes. I pay attention to my surroundings because you have to when you're moving heavy freight all day. If you don't pay attention, you get run over by a forklift or you drop a pallet of glass jars and spend three hours sweeping it up. So I notice things. I notice when people are whispering, I notice when my counts are off, and I notice when a man speaks right into my ear. \n\nBut it's not like I'm sitting around trying to decode hidden meanings in the breakroom bulletin board. I just go to work, I do my job better than anyone else there, and I try to keep my head down until they force me to defend myself. I don't have time to be making things up or looking for messages that aren't there. Between the warehouse and trying to keep the house clean while my husband just sits there telling me I'm crazy, I'm exhausted. So no, just the voice and the sabotage at work. That's more than enough for one person to deal with, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong beliefs or ideas that others told you were not true, but you still felt certain were real, even if there was no proof?\n\nPatient: Well, that is exactly why I am sitting in this chair right now, honestly. I came in because my husband and the warehouse people, they all keep acting like I am completely crazy. Like I am just making things up out of thin air or losing my grip on reality. But I am not. I know exactly what is going on, and I know for an absolute fact that they have been watching my every single move at work. \n\nYou have to understand how a warehouse operates, okay? It is all about your numbers. How much freight you move, how accurate your logs are, how fast you clear the loading dock. And I work harder than anyone else on that floor. I do not stand around drinking energy drinks and gossiping like Dave and Marcus do. I keep my head down and I pull my pallets. And because of that, there is this supervisor spot that is opening up soon. It is a big deal, it means a pay bump, it means better hours, and it means I would actually be in charge of the floor. And they know I deserve it. They know I am the top candidate. \n\nSo, what do they do? They start sabotaging me. I know they have been sneaking around and changing my pallet counts to make me look incompetent. I will count a pallet of freight, log it perfectly, wrap it, and put it in the racks. Then, two hours later, the floor manager comes up to me and says my count is short by three boxes. And I know it wasn't short! I know they went in and changed the tags or moved the boxes just to mess up my accuracy rate. They are doing it specifically to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, because they want it for themselves or they just cannot stand the idea of me telling them what to do.\n\nBut when I try to explain this to my husband, when I tell him that there is this whole coordinated effort against me at work, he just sighs and rolls his eyes at me. He tells me I am being paranoid. He says, Nicole, there is no proof of that, you are just stressed, you are imagining things, why would they risk their jobs just to mess with your pallets? And it makes me so angry because the proof is right there! The proof is that my numbers are suddenly wrong when they have never been wrong before in my life! But he just sides with them, basically. He tells me I need help, that my beliefs are not real, and he acts like I am this crazy person who needs to be fixed. But I am not crazy. I just have coworkers who are actively trying to ruin my livelihood, and a husband who refuses to open his eyes and see it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. And because of that, there is this supervisor spot that is opening up soon. It is a big deal, it means a pay bump, it means better hours, and it means I would actually be in charge of the floor. And they know I deserve it. They know I am the top candidate. \n\nSo, what do they do? They start sabotaging me. I know they have been sneaking around and changing my pallet counts to make me look incompetent. I will count a pallet of freight, log it perfectly, wrap it, and put it in the racks. Then, two hours later, the floor manager comes up to me and says my count is short by three boxes. And I know it wasn't short! I know they went in and changed the tags or moved the boxes just to mess up my accuracy rate. They are doing it specifically to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, because they want it for themselves or they just cannot stand the idea of me telling them what to do.\n\nBut when I try to explain this to my husband, when I tell him that there is this whole coordinated effort against me at work, he just sighs and rolls his eyes at me. He tells me I am being paranoid. He says, Nicole, there is no proof of that, you are just stressed, you are imagining things, why would they risk their jobs just to mess with your pallets? And it makes me so angry because the proof is right there! The proof is that my numbers are suddenly wrong when they have never been wrong before in my life! But he just sides with them, basically. He tells me I need help, that my beliefs are not real, and he acts like I am this crazy person who needs to be fixed. But I am not crazy. I just have coworkers who are actively trying to ruin my livelihood, and a husband who refuses to open his eyes and see it.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been times when you believed something strongly that others told you was not true, not related to work, and yet you still felt certain it was real, even if there was no proof?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / laborer_or_freight_stock_or_material_mover / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my husband and the warehouse people keep acting like I'm crazy, but I know they have been watching my moves and changing the pallet counts to keep me from getting that supervisor spot.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, doctor, no. I don't... I don't know why we're trying to look for something else or change the subject when what's happening right in front of me is already so massive and taking up all my energy. Like I was just trying to explain to you, the entire reason I even walked through the doors of this clinic today, the only reason I came in, is because my husband and the warehouse people just keep acting like I'm crazy. 

You ask about things outside of work, well, my husband is outside of work, and he is right in the middle of this because he refuses to believe me. I go home, my feet are absolutely killing me from standing on concrete for ten hours straight, and I try to tell him what happened. I try to explain that I know for a fact they have been watching my moves all day. I can literally feel their eyes burning into the back of my safety vest when I'm on the forklift. They wait—they literally wait until I go to the bathroom or step off the floor to grab a bottle of water—and they sneak over and change the pallet counts on my clipboard. I have seen the eraser marks! I have seen the numbers changed! 

And they are doing all of this just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It is a deliberate, coordinated attack because they know I'm the best worker there and they can't stand the thought of me being in charge of them. But when I tell my husband this, he just looks at me like I've grown a second head. He tells me there's no proof. He tells me I'm being paranoid and that I need to see a doctor. That is why I am sitting here right now. Because he and the warehouse guys have basically decided I'm crazy, just because I am the only one actually paying attention to what they are doing to me. 

So, no, I don't have any other "beliefs" about things outside of this. I don't need anything else! I just have the absolute truth about how they are sabotaging my promotion, and it is incredibly frustrating that my own husband would rather call me crazy than stand up for me.

He and the people at the warehouse where I work, they just keep acting like I’m crazy. Like I'm making things up or losing my mind. But I'm not. I work freight, you know, moving materials, loading and unloading the trucks, and I’ve been busting my back there for a long time. I know exactly what my numbers are. But at the end of the shift, the logs are always short. They are purposely going in and changing the counts to make my production look bad, just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. I don't have a problem, I just pay attention. If you worked in a warehouse, you'd know how cutthroat it gets, and they know I'd be their boss if they didn't mess with my numbers, so they're all in on it together...\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how you know they are changing your pallet counts? Have you seen anyone actually doing it, or is it based on your observations and feelings?\n\nPatient: Well, I haven't caught them red-handed at the computer, if that's what you mean, but I don't need to. I even started keeping a little tally in my pocket, just making a mark with a Sharpie on a piece of scrap cardboard every time I drop one at the loading dock. The guys on the floor—specifically Dave and Marcus, they're always hanging around the breakroom when I'm out sweating on the floor—they're the ones doing it. I can see them looking at me from the corner of my eye. Like, I'll be coming back from the dock, and they'll be standing by the water cooler, and the second I turn the corner, they stop talking. Just dead silence. And then they give each other this look, this little smirk, and I just know. I know they were just talking about how they bumped my numbers down again. \n\nAnd my husband, he just says, \"Maybe you miscounted, honey,\" or \"Maybe the scanner didn't read the barcode right.\" But he doesn't get it. He works in an office, he doesn't know what it's like on the floor. He thinks I'm just getting worked up over nothing, that I'm, I don't know, paranoid or something. That's why he made me come here, because he thinks I'm acting crazy. The people at work think I'm crazy, too, or at least they want everyone else to think I am, so nobody will believe me when I say I'm the best worker they have. They want that supervisor job for themselves, or for one of their buddies, and they know the only way to stop me from getting it is to make it look like I'm slacking off and losing my mind. But I'm not. I'm just paying attention to what's right in front of my face. I mean, the math doesn't lie, right? My tally marks don't lie. They are definitely changing the counts. I just know it.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people at work are targeting you specifically or treating you differently because of who you are or what you've accomplished?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely they are targeting me specifically! That's exactly what I've been trying to tell my husband, but he just... he just doesn't listen. Like I said, I only came in here today because he and the people at the warehouse keep acting like I'm crazy, like I'm making this whole thing up in my head. But I know for a fact they are watching my every move out on that floor. It's not just a feeling, it's a coordinated effort. \n\nThey know I'm the hardest worker there. I've been doing this since I dropped out in the 11th grade—I know freight, I know how to move material, I know the flow of the dock better than the managers do. And they know that if it was just based on merit, if it was just based on who actually gets the work done, that supervisor spot would be mine in a heartbeat. So yeah, they are treating me differently because of what I've accomplished and what I'm capable of. \n\nThat's why they're changing my pallet counts. They have to. It's the only way they can drag my numbers down to make me look bad on paper. Because if they don't mess with the system, if they don't actively sabotage my production logs, I'm getting that promotion. And Dave and Marcus, they can't stand the thought of taking orders from me. They want one of their own buddies in that spot, someone who will let them take extra-long smoke breaks and slack off by the water cooler. So they target me. They watch me, they whisper about me, and they change my counts to keep me down. It's so clear to me, I just don't understand why no one else will just look at the facts and see it!\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen, heard, or sensed things that others around you don’t seem to notice, like voices, visions, or other experiences that aren’t there for other people?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... it is funny you ask that. Because, see, I do not really like talking about this part. It just gives my husband more ammunition, you know? Like, if I tell him, he will just use it against me and say, see, Nicole, I told you that you are losing it. But... I mean, I guess there is this one thing. It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. \n\nIt is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. \n\nThe first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. And it is always that same man's voice, clear as day, telling me, Nicole, check it again. \n\nIt is so real that I physically turn my whole body around because I expect to bump right into whoever is standing there trying to sneak up on me. And then, like I mentioned, even when I am just at home folding clothes in the living room, it happens the exact same way. It sounds like it is coming from right behind the couch, or just hovering over my shoulder while I am matching up my husband's work socks. And there is nobody near me! Nobody at all. I am completely alone in the room, but the voice is right there in the air. \n\nIf it was just inside my head, why would I be looking behind the laundry basket? Why would I be walking to the end of the warehouse aisle to see who just spoke to me? My husband tries to tell me it is just my inner monologue working overtime because I am stressed out about Dave and Marcus messing with my pallet counts, but your inner monologue does not echo off the metal racking in a warehouse, does it? No, it does not. It is an external sound, completely outside of my body, just hanging there in the room, and it honestly just frustrates me so much that nobody else can hear it when it is that loud and that close to my own two ears.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences where you believed something was happening or someone was communicating with you that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Just the voice, just that man telling me to check it again. And the thing with Dave and Marcus messing with my numbers, but that's not... that's not me believing something that isn't happening, that's just a fact. That's just them being threatened by my work ethic. But as far as other things... no. I don't get secret messages from the TV or the radio or anything like that, if that's what you mean. \n\nI know some people get like that, where they think the news anchor is talking directly to them or they see signs in the license plates on the highway, but I'm not like that. I'm very observant, yes. I pay attention to my surroundings because you have to when you're moving heavy freight all day. If you don't pay attention, you get run over by a forklift or you drop a pallet of glass jars and spend three hours sweeping it up. So I notice things. I notice when people are whispering, I notice when my counts are off, and I notice when a man speaks right into my ear. \n\nBut it's not like I'm sitting around trying to decode hidden meanings in the breakroom bulletin board. I just go to work, I do my job better than anyone else there, and I try to keep my head down until they force me to defend myself. I don't have time to be making things up or looking for messages that aren't there. Between the warehouse and trying to keep the house clean while my husband just sits there telling me I'm crazy, I'm exhausted. So no, just the voice and the sabotage at work. That's more than enough for one person to deal with, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong beliefs or ideas that others told you were not true, but you still felt certain were real, even if there was no proof?\n\nPatient: Well, that is exactly why I am sitting in this chair right now, honestly. I came in because my husband and the warehouse people, they all keep acting like I am completely crazy. Like I am just making things up out of thin air or losing my grip on reality. But I am not. I know exactly what is going on, and I know for an absolute fact that they have been watching my every single move at work. \n\nYou have to understand how a warehouse operates, okay? It is all about your numbers. How much freight you move, how accurate your logs are, how fast you clear the loading dock. And I work harder than anyone else on that floor. I do not stand around drinking energy drinks and gossiping like Dave and Marcus do. I keep my head down and I pull my pallets. And because of that, there is this supervisor spot that is opening up soon. It is a big deal, it means a pay bump, it means better hours, and it means I would actually be in charge of the floor. And they know I deserve it. They know I am the top candidate. \n\nSo, what do they do? They start sabotaging me. I know they have been sneaking around and changing my pallet counts to make me look incompetent. I will count a pallet of freight, log it perfectly, wrap it, and put it in the racks. Then, two hours later, the floor manager comes up to me and says my count is short by three boxes. And I know it wasn't short! I know they went in and changed the tags or moved the boxes just to mess up my accuracy rate. They are doing it specifically to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, because they want it for themselves or they just cannot stand the idea of me telling them what to do.\n\nBut when I try to explain this to my husband, when I tell him that there is this whole coordinated effort against me at work, he just sighs and rolls his eyes at me. He tells me I am being paranoid. He says, Nicole, there is no proof of that, you are just stressed, you are imagining things, why would they risk their jobs just to mess with your pallets? And it makes me so angry because the proof is right there! The proof is that my numbers are suddenly wrong when they have never been wrong before in my life! But he just sides with them, basically. He tells me I need help, that my beliefs are not real, and he acts like I am this crazy person who needs to be fixed. But I am not crazy. I just have coworkers who are actively trying to ruin my livelihood, and a husband who refuses to open his eyes and see it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that others told you was not true, not related to work, and yet you still felt certain it was real, even if there was no proof?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doctor, no. It is not like I am seeing ghosts or anything like that, because I am completely grounded in reality. But sometimes... like, if I am out in the aisle at the warehouse, way in the back where the overstock is, or even just at home, doing something totally normal like folding clothes in the living room... I will hear this voice. \n\nIt is a man's voice. And it is clear as day. It is not a mumble or just some random thought in my head, it sounds exactly like a real person standing right behind my shoulder. And he always says the exact same thing to me. He says, Nicole, check it again. \n\nThe first few times it happened at the warehouse, I actually spun around, thinking it was Dave or one of the managers trying to sneak up on me and catch me making a mistake with my freight. Because, like I told you, they are always watching me and trying to mess with my counts. So I would whip around, ready to tell them to back off, but... there is nobody there. Not a single soul down the whole aisle. Just empty racking. And then it started happening at home, too. I will be matching socks or folding my work shirts, just completely alone in the room, and suddenly I hear, Nicole, check it again. And I will go check the front door, or I will go check my pallet tally marks from my pocket, because the voice is so incredibly real. But my husband, he will be sitting right there in the next room, and he does not hear a thing. He just gives me that look when I ask him who was just talking. But I know what I heard. I mean, it is so loud and clear, I do not get how he or anyone else does not hear it when it happens, even when nobody is near me.\n\nDoctor: When you hear the voice saying 'Nicole, check it again,' does it sound like it's coming from inside your head or from somewhere outside, like from another room or from behind you?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, it is definitely, one hundred percent coming from outside of me. It is not inside my head at all. I know what my own thoughts sound like, right? Everybody has thoughts. But this... this is out in the open air. Like, when I am in the aisle at work, way down in the overstock section where it gets kind of echoey, it sounds exactly like a physical person is standing maybe two feet behind my left shoulder. I can almost hear the breath before the words. And it is always that same man's voice, clear as day, telling me, Nicole, check it again. \n\nIt is so real that I physically turn my whole body around because I expect to bump right into whoever is standing there trying to sneak up on me. And then, like I mentioned, even when I am just at home folding clothes in the living room, it happens the exact same way. It sounds like it is coming from right behind the couch, or just hovering over my shoulder while I am matching up my husband's work socks. And there is nobody near me! Nobody at all. I am completely alone in the room, but the voice is right there in the air. \n\nIf it was just inside my head, why would I be looking behind the laundry basket? Why would I be walking to the end of the warehouse aisle to see who just spoke to me? My husband tries to tell me it is just my inner monologue working overtime because I am stressed out about Dave and Marcus messing with my pallet counts, but your inner monologue does not echo off the metal racking in a warehouse, does it? No, it does not. It is an external sound, completely outside of my body, just hanging there in the room, and it honestly just frustrates me so much that nobody else can hear it when it is that loud and that close to my own two ears.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other experiences where you believed something was happening or someone was communicating with you that others didn’t seem to notice or agree with?\n\nPatient: I mean... no, not really. Just the voice, just that man telling me to check it again. And the thing with Dave and Marcus messing with my numbers, but that's not... that's not me believing something that isn't happening, that's just a fact. That's just them being threatened by my work ethic. But as far as other things... no. I don't get secret messages from the TV or the radio or anything like that, if that's what you mean. \n\nI know some people get like that, where they think the news anchor is talking directly to them or they see signs in the license plates on the highway, but I'm not like that. I'm very observant, yes. I pay attention to my surroundings because you have to when you're moving heavy freight all day. If you don't pay attention, you get run over by a forklift or you drop a pallet of glass jars and spend three hours sweeping it up. So I notice things. I notice when people are whispering, I notice when my counts are off, and I notice when a man speaks right into my ear. \n\nBut it's not like I'm sitting around trying to decode hidden meanings in the breakroom bulletin board. I just go to work, I do my job better than anyone else there, and I try to keep my head down until they force me to defend myself. I don't have time to be making things up or looking for messages that aren't there. Between the warehouse and trying to keep the house clean while my husband just sits there telling me I'm crazy, I'm exhausted. So no, just the voice and the sabotage at work. That's more than enough for one person to deal with, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any strong beliefs or ideas that others told you were not true, but you still felt certain were real, even if there was no proof?\n\nPatient: Well, that is exactly why I am sitting in this chair right now, honestly. I came in because my husband and the warehouse people, they all keep acting like I am completely crazy. Like I am just making things up out of thin air or losing my grip on reality. But I am not. I know exactly what is going on, and I know for an absolute fact that they have been watching my every single move at work. \n\nYou have to understand how a warehouse operates, okay? It is all about your numbers. How much freight you move, how accurate your logs are, how fast you clear the loading dock. And I work harder than anyone else on that floor. I do not stand around drinking energy drinks and gossiping like Dave and Marcus do. I keep my head down and I pull my pallets. And because of that, there is this supervisor spot that is opening up soon. It is a big deal, it means a pay bump, it means better hours, and it means I would actually be in charge of the floor. And they know I deserve it. They know I am the top candidate. \n\nSo, what do they do? They start sabotaging me. I know they have been sneaking around and changing my pallet counts to make me look incompetent. I will count a pallet of freight, log it perfectly, wrap it, and put it in the racks. Then, two hours later, the floor manager comes up to me and says my count is short by three boxes. And I know it wasn't short! I know they went in and changed the tags or moved the boxes just to mess up my accuracy rate. They are doing it specifically to keep me from getting that supervisor spot, because they want it for themselves or they just cannot stand the idea of me telling them what to do.\n\nBut when I try to explain this to my husband, when I tell him that there is this whole coordinated effort against me at work, he just sighs and rolls his eyes at me. He tells me I am being paranoid. He says, Nicole, there is no proof of that, you are just stressed, you are imagining things, why would they risk their jobs just to mess with your pallets? And it makes me so angry because the proof is right there! The proof is that my numbers are suddenly wrong when they have never been wrong before in my life! But he just sides with them, basically. He tells me I need help, that my beliefs are not real, and he acts like I am this crazy person who needs to be fixed. But I am not crazy. I just have coworkers who are actively trying to ruin my livelihood, and a husband who refuses to open his eyes and see it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly that others told you was not true, not related to work, and yet you still felt certain it was real, even if there was no proof?\n\nPatient: Honestly, doctor, no. I don't... I don't know why we're trying to look for something else or change the subject when what's happening right in front of me is already so massive and taking up all my energy. Like I was just trying to explain to you, the entire reason I even walked through the doors of this clinic today, the only reason I came in, is because my husband and the warehouse people just keep acting like I'm crazy. \n\nYou ask about things outside of work, well, my husband is outside of work, and he is right in the middle of this because he refuses to believe me. I go home, my feet are absolutely killing me from standing on concrete for ten hours straight, and I try to tell him what happened. I try to explain that I know for a fact they have been watching my moves all day. I can literally feel their eyes burning into the back of my safety vest when I'm on the forklift. They wait—they literally wait until I go to the bathroom or step off the floor to grab a bottle of water—and they sneak over and change the pallet counts on my clipboard. I have seen the eraser marks! I have seen the numbers changed! \n\nAnd they are doing all of this just to keep me from getting that supervisor spot. It is a deliberate, coordinated attack because they know I'm the best worker there and they can't stand the thought of me being in charge of them. But when I tell my husband this, he just looks at me like I've grown a second head. He tells me there's no proof. He tells me I'm being paranoid and that I need to see a doctor. That is why I am sitting here right now. Because he and the warehouse guys have basically decided I'm crazy, just because I am the only one actually paying attention to what they are doing to me. \n\nSo, no, I don't have any other \"beliefs\" about things outside of this. I don't need anything else! I just have the absolute truth about how they are sabotaging my promotion, and it is incredibly frustrating that my own husband would rather call me crazy than stand up for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "role": "user",
